- **2026-10-15 14:00 CST (Claude, maple-pc): PZ closing at startup is FIXED in source, built and installed; verified that PZ stays open on its main menu with PzCraft's start screen drawn (window capture). Not run past the menu; Minecraft not started.** Owner report: "PZ closes at startup, before the menu". Cause (pz-launch.log, 21:46): `FATAL ERROR in native method ... No context is current` at `ImGuiImplGl3.init` → `PzUi.create` → `PzUi.frameEnd` → `Display.imguiEndFrame` on the main loop thread (no GL context there). Fix: `Patch_PzUiFrame` now hooks `Display.update(boolean)` OnEnter (the render-thread frame swap, same point as Viewpoint's `Patch_FrameSwap` and our `Patch_DisplayUpdate`), and `PzUi.glReady()` refuses to touch GL until `glfwGetCurrentContext()` and `GL.getCapabilities()` are valid. - **Public-user fixes (2026-10-04 22:45, Claude) — code verified; published at 22:55, see the entry above:** three reports from players of the public release. (1) Minecraft music audible: **verified** — `PzCraftClient` sets the MUSIC slider to 0 with the other forced options. (2) Minecraft kept running after PZ quit: **verified** — auto-exit is now the default instead of needing `PZCRAFT_AUTOEXIT`; Minecraft stops itself (orderly, world saved) ~3 s after PZ's process ends, 20 s if PZ still runs but stops beating; opt out with `-Dpzcraft.autoexit=false` or `PZCRAFT_AUTOEXIT=0`; new `SharedLink.peerProcessAlive()`. (3) One player's Minecraft "started normally": **diagnosed** from `crash-user-log/message.txt` — their Prism instance has no Fabric Loader component (main class is vanilla `net.minecraft.client.main.Main`, resource packs `vanilla, vanilla`), so no mod loaded; the fix is the README (explicit Fabric Loader step + Troubleshooting section). ## 2. Shared handoff routine

### When starting a session

1. Read sections 1–5 and the relevant roadmap/source-map section.
2. Verify the current user request. Manual play takes precedence over the development backlog. Check `pzctl.py state` and process inventory before any input, restart, install or fixture.
3. Compare current source/build/evidence with this snapshot. Resolve stale documentation using source, exact build results, installed hashes and test artifacts.
4. Choose one bounded next step. Record it in section 1, including what will count as success.

### While working and before a limit/handoff

- Update this file after each meaningful build, install, test, decision or failure. Do not wait until the usage limit is exhausted; an abrupt cutoff may leave no final turn.
- Keep section 1 short and current. Append a dated entry to section 10 with agent, task, changed files, exact checks/results, install state, backup location and next action.
- Use distinct statuses: **planned**, **implemented**, **built**, **installed**, **verified**, **failed**, **unverified**, or **inconclusive**. Building is not gameplay verification; a partial success inside a failed test is not an overall pass.
- Identify the exact artifact/build a result applies to. Preserve failed evidence; use a fresh filename or timestamp when a rerun would overwrite useful history.
- Before handing the game to the owner, release synthetic input, stop tests, and clearly state whether it is paused, running or closed. Do not change manual play to make the handoff look tidier.
- Record temporary settings, fixtures, inventory changes and cleanup failures. Preserve the owner's blocks, items and save changes.
- No automatic task, monitor or usage-limit watcher was created. This is a shared file workflow; neither agent can rely on receiving a final message from an agent that has already hit its limit.

Suggested entry:

```text
### YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM CST — Agent — task
Changed: paths and resulting behavior.
Build: commands, success/failure, source/build identity.
Installed: yes/no; installed hash or reason not installed.
Validation: exact artifact(s), assertions, overall result, cleanup.
Game/save: current processes/save/pause/input; observation time.
Backup: path and what it contains.
Unfinished: precise failure or uncertainty.
Next: one concrete step, with success criteria.
```

## 3. Owner decisions and boundaries

- **Updated 2026-10-04 20:58 CST:** owner confirmed updated distribution permission for a public release of original PzCraft source/binaries. The sanitized release excludes private references, extracted game assets, and licensed Point Blank files and credits Viewpoint and Vic Point Blank. The prior private-only boundary is historical.
- PZ is the main world, rendered by Viewpoint. A real Minecraft client supplies Steve's physics, blocks, inventory and combat. PZ owns its world, native actors and time; Minecraft owns Steve-mode health/death, hunger and saturation. PZ survival stats and thermal state return on exit from Steve mode.
- The chosen inventory design is **Minecraft ownership while Steve drives**, with a future hidden PZ mirror for keys, native interaction and crafting. The complete mirror/reconcile is still pending.
- Past development sessions allowed launches/restarts and checks in the backed-up `PzCraftFeatureTest-20261002` save. **Latest steering:** the owner is testing manually. A roadmap item is not permission to interrupt that session; follow the current request.
- Announce game launches or actions that take desktop focus. Prefer the local control API for test input; do not commandeer keyboard/mouse for an unannounced automated session.
- The owner judged the remaining faint third-person temporal trail negligible. **Do not spend more work on it.** The doubled avatar was a separate bug fixed in 0.12.
- The owner supplied Point Blank Fabric 26.1.2 v2.2.0 and reports permission to decompile/use **gun mechanics, models and animations only**. No complete-mod copying, gun building/crafting, benches, recipes or trading. PZ supplies guns/ammo/stats/noise; use PZ sounds. Gun sound assets were not extracted.
- Existing authorization does not require repeated permission questions for ordinary work within the requested scope. Preserve explicit boundaries and current manual-play intent.
- **2026-10-04 13:21 owner steering:** "i dont want you to waiting for approval. can you just do it yourself? also resume the tasks and bugs fixes to do". Agents may handle approvals for their own verified PzCraft builds. Run `tools/pz-mcp/approve-pzcraft.py` after install and before launch; it verifies built = installed SHA256, backs up and adds only that PzCraft hash to private `~/.pzcraft/zb-dev/mod_approvals.json`. Keep normal `prompt` policy and the owner's ordinary profile. This supersedes older requests for a human click on each PzCraft rebuild. Manual-play checks, save/backup rules, private references and download boundaries still apply.

- **2026-10-15 owner steering: guns are paused.** "i'm gonna have to stop with guns. for now i must focus on playability of pz and mc" / "there numerous things to do consider before we can continue this guns shenaginans". No gun models, clips or gun bugs (B2) until the owner reopens them; work goes to PZ/Minecraft playability.

### Expanded container failure: resolved 2026-10-04 (kept for the record)

The 10-02 22:55 record (`containers-object-results-20261002-225537-FAILED-contaminated-fixture.json`) failed because the test's put-back step moved every matching stack Steve carried into the container, including leftovers from earlier aborted runs. It was not a bridge defect. The fixed script moves only slots that filled during its own take, refuses to continue if a stack merged into an existing one, and records `*AlreadyCarried`/`*InventoryRestored`. The rerun passed (see the ledger and journal). Do not reintroduce "move everything matching in the player's inventory" or blanket `mc clear` cleanup in any inventory test: Steve's pockets hold the owner's items.

### Existing protocol reports

`protocol/build/test-results/test/TEST-*.xml` records **40 tests, zero failures/errors/skips**, timestamp 2026-10-02 22:49 CST. Classes: CollisionTest, EntityLinkTest, GuiEventTest, InputReaderTest, MaterialSectionTest, ProtocolTest, PzExplosionTest, SceneLinkTest, VehicleHullTest, WeatherTest, WorldClockTest and WorldObjectsTest.

These are existing reports, not a new test run. They do not establish container/item-state correctness. Earlier documents reported 23/26/30/31 tests for earlier builds; those counts are historical, not contradictory current totals.

## 6. Forward plan

### H — finish unified inventory before weapon integration

1. **Close the expanded-container failure.** Reproduce using an isolated fixture without inherited inventory contamination. Preserve result identities and expected counts. Success: whole test passed=true, exact returns, no additional/vanished stacks, clean cleanup.
2. **Verify new sources and whole-item preservation.** Furniture, two-container fridge/freezer, corpse and floor sources; damaged/named items; age/cooked state; remaining uses; bag-with-contents, full water bottle and bulletproof vest. Check both directions and restart persistence. Large byte payloads require bounded transport and explicit overflow/failure handling.
3. **Verify item use.** Apple fills MC hunger and decrements one item; appropriate canned food leaves its empty container; bulletproof vest equips and adds armor; a T-shirt equips without adding armor. Exercise stale/rotten food and age while carried. Full PZ effects for drinks, alcohol, poison and medicine still need design/implementation and evidence.
4. **Complete ownership.** MC is authoritative while Steve drives; keep a hidden PZ inventory equal so keys/car keys/native recipes can work. Represent MC-only items in PZ using a private `PzCraft.MCItem` wrapper with id/components in mod data. Reconcile on mode entry/exit and persistence/reconnect. Do not transfer ownership by silently destroying the old inventory.
**H4 design note (Claude, 2026-10-04 — owner chose "mirror"; this is the design being built, status: planned until the entries in §10 say otherwise).**

- *Facts it is built on:* the hidden PZ character's pockets and Steve's Minecraft inventory are today two unrelated sets (PZ: `IDcard` + `KeyRing` holding one key; MC: 28 stacks). Both games save independently. Items already travel as `pzcraft:pz_item` stacks (type + state, whole-item bytes `b` for stateful ones, PZ item id `i`); everything else is Minecraft-only.
- *Authority:* Minecraft is canonical while Steve drives (owner rule). The PZ inventory is a projection of it, **plus** PZ-originated changes that are carried to Minecraft as acknowledged transactions (crafting, key use, items picked up in PZ-only play). One merge formula covers every case: `target = MC_state + (PZ_now − ledger)` per item key, clamped at 0, where `ledger` is the multiset PZ last conformed to, **persisted in the same PZ save as the inventory** (player modData `PzCraftMirror`). A crash on either side therefore rolls back consistently instead of duplicating: a lost MC state means PZ conforms to the older MC state; a PZ rollback restores ledger and inventory together.
- *First sync (ledger empty) is a union:* Steve receives the PZ items (`IDcard`, `KeyRing`+key), PZ receives Steve's items. Nothing is silently destroyed. If the ledger is missing but mirror wrappers exist, `ledger := PZ_now` (Minecraft wins) and it is logged loudly.
- *Item identity:* group key = `type|c|k|u|nm|i` (condition, cooked, uses, custom name, PZ item id); **age is excluded** (ageing is informational; PZ-side ageing must not look like a change). Minecraft-only stack key = `mc|sha1(SNBT of the stack at count 1)`. Same key function on both sides (protocol module, unit-tested). Stateful PZ items change in place (bag contents, fluid, condition): PZ detects that with a cheap structural signature kept only in its ledger and sends a *replace* that Minecraft applies **in the same slot** (hotbar layout is never shuffled).
- *PZ representation of Minecraft-only stacks:* one private PZ item `PzCraft.MCItem` per key (script in `media/scripts`), modData `{k, n, snbt}`, name like "TNT x59". Removing the wrapper in PZ removes the whole Minecraft group; changing the count in Minecraft updates the wrapper in place.
- *Messages (new ids):* 66 `MIRROR_STATE` MC→PZ (full canonical state as groups, blobs only for keys PZ has not been sent this session, plus `ack` = recently applied tx ids); 67 `MIRROR_TX` PZ→MC (`add`/`rem`/`rep` + id); 68 `MIRROR_HELLO` PZ→MC (new link epoch: reset what has been sent). One transaction in flight; PZ persists it until acked and resends it; Minecraft remembers applied ids (persisted) so a resend is never applied twice.
- *Rules:* Minecraft applies adds with `placeItemBackInInventory` (if the inventory is full it drops the stack at Steve's feet: never destroyed); removes clamp; slots/hotbar of untouched stacks never move. Items PZ cannot represent (type not in the catalogue, blob too large) are not mirrored and stay Minecraft-only (counted in stats). Worn/equipped state is **not** mirrored in this stage (items sit unworn in the PZ pockets). Armor/offhand stacks count as carried. Death/respawn: Minecraft drops the inventory as vanilla does; the mirror follows.
- *Out of scope here:* PZ capacity rules, floor/drop lifecycle, trunks, worn-state mapping, MCItem crafting use.
- *Success (evidence required before calling it done):* mixed inventory visible in PZ (keys, bag, MCItem wrappers) with exact states; PZ-side craft/consume/add flows to Minecraft exactly once; PZ-only play changes merge on the next link-up; save → restart both → identical on both sides; killed-PZ and killed-MC cases do not duplicate; no hotbar shuffling; zero failures in both `stats`.

5. **Container rules.** Support car trunks; generate unexplored PZ loot with native rules; enforce PZ weight capacity. The current chest screen shows at most six rows and only PZ items may be deposited; unshown items remain untouched. Test overflow and concurrent changes explicitly.
6. **Floor/drop lifecycle.** Planned: walking pickup for PZ floor items; dropping a PZ stack creates a saved native PZ floor item; MC stacks remain MC entities. Current floor looting via a chest screen does not complete this.
7. **Condition/tools/equivalents.** Wire real condition wear and tool behavior rather than tooltip-only numbers. Plain equivalents (logs/planks/apple etc.) converting to native MC items are a roadmap idea, not a current pass. PZ crafting/building follows the ownership bridge.
8. **Declare the milestone only after evidence.** Mixed PZ/MC inventory, mode switches, save/reload, death/respawn, keys, no duplication/loss, clean UI restoration. Decide/version the 0.14 label when the implementation is actually ready.

### I — scoped melee and guns

1. Implement PZ melee stacks with PZ script stats; map SwingAnim/categories/two-handed type to appropriate Better Combat behavior, retain MaxHitcount, condition wear, tree-tool tags and proxy hits. Library compatibility and current API must be verified before integration; no Better Combat dependency is currently a completion claim.
2. Start with **PZ Base.Pistol ↔ Point Blank M9** in a separate rendering spike. Confirm MC 26.3 first-person draw/reload/fire, third-person model, server shots and animations before expanding the weapon list. Do not install the complete supplied mod into the live development instance or use a version override as proof of compatibility.
3. Preserve scoped mechanics: single/burst/auto firing phases, elapsed-time/RPM scheduling (not rendered frame count), authoritative shot/reload state and prediction reconciliation, per-stack gun identity, draw/hide and weapon-swap handling.
4. PZ data supplies capacity, damage, ammo, spread, reload and shot sound/noise. Map PZ pistols/revolvers/shotguns/rifles to allowed visuals; ammunition includes 9mm, .45, .44, .38, .357, 5.56, .308, .30-30 and shotgun shells. Ammo boxes unpack into rounds; magazine simplification needs a recorded decision.
5. Add PZ effects: shot noise attracts native zombies; bullets damage PZ windows/doors/cars and proxy actors. Keep ordinary MC weapons working on native actors. Reverse-mode PZ use of MC weapons is later work.
6. Evidence required: multi-target axe with capped hits, M9 firing/reload/ammo consumption, noise response, window impact, condition wear, swap/reconnect behavior and stable rendering. The old roadmap's `check-weapons.py` is a proposed test, not evidence of an existing completed implementation.

### All PZ guns (owner request 2026-10-06: "then we start implementing all pz guns")

PZ has 22 ranged weapons (`Item.isAimedFirearm`): pistols Pistol (M9), Pistol2 (M1911 .45), Pistol3 (Desert Eagle .44); revolvers Revolver (.357), Revolver_Long (.44), Revolver_Short (.38) and the toy Revolver_CapGun; shotguns Shotgun (pump, 5), ShotgunSawnoff, JS3T_Shotgun (7), DoubleBarrelShotgun, DoubleBarrelShotgunSawnoff (all 9 pellets); rifles AssaultRifle (M16, 556Clip 30, Auto/Single), AssaultRifle2 (M14, M14Clip 20), JS14_Rifle (20), TrapperCarbine (.45 clip, 7), HuntingRifle / MSR7T_Rifle (.308 bolt, 4, rack after shot), VarmintRifle (.223 bolt, 5), L92_Carbine (.357 lever, 10), L94_Rifle (.30-30 lever, 6), Rifle_CapGun. Everything below is taken from PZ's own scripts through `ItemExporter.firearm` (items.json `weapon.firearm/reloadType/magazine/ammoKey/projectiles/spread/rackAfterShoot/haveChamber/insertAll/needsClose/manualSpent/piercing/fireMode/recoilDelay/aimingTime/jam/ammoPerShoot`; catalogue version 2).

**Design.** PZ's `ISReloadWeaponAction`/`ISRackFirearm`/`onShoot` rules are reproduced in `GunBridge` for every firearm: a shot is `onShoot` (chamber, spent round, ammo, jam), a rack-after-shoot gun (pump, bolt, lever) is worked after each shot (busy ~0.85 s, rack sound, `racked`), a gun with a magazine swaps magazines (R; shift+R takes it out; an empty one drops by itself), a gun without one takes rounds one at a time at the reload type's own pace (shotgun .833 s, revolver .95 s, bolt-no-mag .59 s, double barrel 2.5 s all at once, divided by the Reloading skill's speed; shooting, a swap or R again stops it; a loaded gun with an empty chamber is racked afterwards), shift+R on such a gun unloads one round per loop, revolver spent cases (`manualSpent`) are ejected when reloading starts. `GunTypes` (PZ side) and `PzGuns.firearm/ammoOfMagazine` (Fabric side, from the catalogue) replace the old "Base.Pistol" constants. Several targets per shot: `GunAction.targets` (protocol) carries the other distinct actors a shot reached; PZ rolls each one (cap = the weapon's `MaxHitcount`); the client casts `projectiles` rays (first at the crosshair, the rest in a cone of `spread` x `pellet.spreadScale` (6) degrees) and, for piercing bullets, collects bodies along the ray. Effects: smoke/chips/debris per surface (fewer per pellet), blood per hurt actor, up to 4 tracers.
**Visuals.** The M9 keeps Point Blank's GeckoLib model and clips. Every other gun is drawn from PZ's own mesh through the `pzcraft:pzmesh` special renderer in its `gun` mode (barrel along the mesh's +Y, scaled by its length; placement `pzgun.*` / `pzgun3.*`, ADS sight alignment `pzgun.sight*`/`adsX/adsY`) with a procedural hand animation (`GunMeshAnim`: kick, draw, reload, round loading, rack stroke, inspect, sprint pose) timed by PZ's answers (`seconds`, `per`, `rackLead/rackSeconds`). Point Blank has analogs for most guns (m1911a1, deserteagle, m16a1, mk14ebr, m870/m590, l96a1/srs, rhino, ...): using their models and animations (private, allow-listed, hash-pinned like the M9's) is the next visual step; PZ meshes exist for all but the M9, JS3T and the cap guns.
**Per-gun pass (owner, 2026-10-08): the procedure worked out on the M9, gun by gun.** For a magazine-fed gun: (1) the slide/action is racked BY HAND (R with rounds in the magazine and none in the chamber, and at the end of an empty reload), (2) shift+R has an `unload` clip that takes the magazine out, (3) its magazine is a 3D model OF ITS OWN (owner: "they cant share same model of magazine": built from that gun's own magazine, with that calibre's cartridge), (4) rounds are fed into it by hand with a clip, (5) an empty magazine shows its follower and no round, (6) **added by the owner after the three pistols were marked done:** the gun with no magazine in it has states of its own (`idlenomag`, `drawnomag`, `reloadnomag`, `inspectnomag`, `runningnomag`, `racknomag`; here also `reloademptynomag`): in source for all five magazine kinds, not yet looked at by the owner. "Done" in this table is the owner's word; **"in source" = built and previewed offline on the office PC, never run in the game.**

| Gun | Rack by hand | Empty reload ends with the rack | `unload` clip | Own magazine model + feeding + empty state | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| M9 Pistol (`Base.Pistol`, kind `m9`) | yes (own press check) | yes | yes | `mag9mm` for `Base.9mmClip`, 9x19 | **marked done by the owner 2026-10-08** from the offline viewer; in source, not yet seen in game |
| M1911 Pistol (`Base.Pistol2`, kind `m1911a1`) | yes (own press check) | yes | yes | `mag45` for `Base.45Clip`, .45 ACP (also the Trapper Carbine's magazine) | **marked done by the owner 2026-10-08** from the offline viewer; in source, not yet seen in game |
| B-F Pistol (`Base.Pistol3`, kind `deserteagle`) | yes (from its own slingshot) | its own clip already pulls the slide by hand: unchanged | yes | `mag44` for `Base.44Clip`, .44 Magnum (rimmed); its inspect keeps the right hand on the grip | **marked done by the owner 2026-10-08** from the offline viewer; in source, not yet seen in game |
| M16 (`Base.AssaultRifle`, kind `m16a1`) | yes (charging handle; the pull is Point Blank's own M4 SOPMOD II stroke) | `reloadempty` (a magazine in the gun) hits the bolt release: kept, and the bolt goes home under that hit; **`reloademptynomag` ends with the charging handle pulled (owner 2026-10-09: "should be racking not pressing button")**; the bolt stands back on an empty gun | yes (now ends in the idle hand pose) | `mag556` for `Base.556Clip`, 5.56 (two columns, follower with a rib) | in source 2026-10-08, shown in the offline viewer; **not yet marked by the owner**; not seen in game |
| M1A, JS-14, Trapper (kind `mk14ebr`) | no | not looked at | no (its reload flings the magazine) | no (`Base.M14Clip`, `Base.JS14_Clip`) | open |

**2026-10-15: the four rows above (M9, M1911, B-F, M16) describe the old Point Blank-based pass.** These four guns and their magazines are now our own models with our own clips, in the jar set (journal entries 2026-10-15 15:47 to 19:47). The same per-gun procedure applies: hand rack, `unload`, the own magazine with feeding and an empty state, the no-magazine states.

**For every kind (owner, 2026-10-09; in source, offline preview only):** the idle clips (`idle`, `idleempty`, `idlenomag`) are a 3.0 s loop with a breath on the `main` bone (`build.py` `breathing`, `BREATH_*`), faded out by `VicGunItem` while aiming. A magazine in the hand is held at about a pistol's distance (`MAGAZINE_VIEW`); its `load`, `unloadround` and `draw` clips take the round's pickup point and the draw's start from that view (`under_view`), so moving the view again keeps both out of sight.

**Status.** Stage 1 (generic mechanics: magazine guns, rounds guns, rack, unload) **implemented, installed (PZ `4f63423f…`, Fabric `ec583e4f…`), mechanics verified per gun with `check-guns.py`** (see the journal for the per-gun list). Stage 2 (pellets/piercing, GunMeshAnim, mesh ADS) **implemented and compile-checked, not installed**. Open: auto fire + select fire (M16: B key, hold to fire; PZ cadence unknown), loose-round loading of a magazine for non-9mm (the R-with-magazine-in-hand path works from the catalogue but is untested for others), ammo boxes, JS3T/cap-gun meshes, Point Blank analog models/animations, flash/casings/third-person for mesh guns, mesh placement tuning for all 20 meshes, recoil per gun (scaled from `RecoilDelay`), HUD fire mode, durability/jam behaviour per gun.

### W — Minecraft hands on PZ's world (owner request 2026-10-16; PLAN ONLY, nothing implemented)

Owner (2026-10-16): "apparently competition has feature of minecraft interacting with pz world. we gonna need these features": mineable pebbles (drop cobblestone), grass and bushes; mineable walls, furniture, fences, doors (furniture gives the furniture item, which can be placed again); drivable vehicles with windows broken by punching; Minecraft entities such as arrows colliding with PZ (today arrows pass through PZ walls); usable seats, beds and couches. Status: **approved by the owner 2026-10-16 with the defaults below ("defaults are good"). W1, W0 + W2a (natural things), W2b (edges), W2c + W3 (furniture), W4 (seats, beds) and W5 (cars) implemented, not run in game (journal 2026-10-16 12:07, 12:27, 12:35, 12:50, 12:57, 13:05).**

**What exists to build on (read 2026-10-16).** PZ walls, furniture, floors and car hulls reach Minecraft as collision boxes, not blocks (`CollisionField`, fed by `WorldExporter`; `EntityCollisionMixin` adds them to entity movement). `EntityPickMixin` makes the crosshair hit them as a block hit on the air voxel in front, so left-click on a PZ wall today "mines" air and nothing happens. `PzMaterials` already classifies every tile's floor, edges (wall/door/window/fence + material) and solid object. Trees are the one mineable PZ thing (invisible `PzTreeBlock` stacks, `MSG_TREE_FELLED`, PZ topples the tree, Minecraft drops logs). `ExplosionBridge` (PZ) already removes doors, walls, fences, window frames, furniture and floors the sledgehammer way. `InteractionBridge` already sends doors/windows/containers near Steve every 200 ms (`MSG_OBJECTS`). Cars are only hulls (`VehicleBridge`); `SteveControl` hands control back to PZ when the PZ player is in a car. Arrows miss PZ geometry because projectiles test only blocks: `CollisionGetter.clipIncludingBorder`, called in exactly three places (`ProjectileUtil.getHitResult`, `ProjectileUtil.getHitEntitiesAlong`, `AbstractArrow.tick`).

**PZ facts found in `ref/pz-src` (B42.21):** grass = `IsoObject.isGrass()` (`e_newgrass_*`, `blends_grassoverlays_*`) and `isGrassLike()`; PZ's "Remove grass" uses `IsoFlagType.canBeRemoved`, "Remove bush" `canBeCut`, `isBush()`; loose stones are ground-cover objects whose CustomName maps to an item through the Lua table `GroundCoverItems`; boulders/ores `isOres()` (`boulders_*`, `crafting_ore_*`, FlintBoulder, LimestoneBoulder). Furniture that PZ can pick up has the `IsMoveAble` property; PZ's pickup/place is Lua (`ISMoveableSpriteProps`, not on the VPS: confirm names on maple); the item is `inventory.types.Moveable` (`ReadFromWorldSprite`). Seats: `zombie.seating.SeatingManager` (per-sprite sit positions, `getTranslation`, `getFacingDirection`, `getTilePositionCount(object)`); beds: `IsoFlagType.bed`. Cars: `BaseVehicle.enter(seat, chr)` / `exit(chr)` / `getBestSeat`, `VehicleWindow.hit(chr)` / `damage(int)` / `isHittable()`.

**Design.**
- **W0, targets (shared by W2-W5).** PZ sends, with the 200 ms object snapshot, the things near Steve that Minecraft can act on: kind (grass, bush, stone, boulder/ore, furniture, wall, door, window, fence, seat, bed, car window), material, tile + level + edge, a hit box, and a key that survives snapshots (tile, level, kind, edge, sprite name: runtime ids differ between the PZ client and a PZ server). The crosshair ray includes these boxes (grass and stones have no collision box today).
- **W1, projectiles stop at PZ geometry.** Wrap the three `clipIncludingBorder` calls so the ray also tests `CollisionField` (walls, closed doors and windows, furniture, floors, car hulls); a PZ hit becomes a block hit on the air voxel. Arrows then stick in PZ walls by vanilla's own rules (`lastState` = air never changes, so they stay); add a check that drops them when PZ's box is gone. Also `LivingEntity.hasLineOfSight`, so skeletons don't see or shoot through PZ walls. Optional: an arrow that hits a PZ window smashes it.
- **W2, mining PZ things.** Holding attack on a target runs vanilla's mining sum against a stand-in Minecraft block per material (planks for wood, bricks, stone, iron block for metal, glass, wool for fabric, short grass, a bush, cobblestone for stones), so the right tool, Efficiency, Haste, fatigue and being off the ground all count as in vanilla; a hardness per kind (a wall is tougher than a fence); swing, hit sounds and crack particles of the stand-in block; a progress indicator, because Minecraft cannot draw cracks on air. When done, the Minecraft server checks reach and sends `MSG_PZ_MINE`; PZ removes the object the PZ way (the `ExplosionBridge` routines, contents spilled) and answers with what to drop; Minecraft pops the drops. Works the same in single player and in the host pair (Minecraft server ↔ PZ server).
- **W2 drops (defaults, owner may change):** grass: wheat seeds 1 in 8 (vanilla's short grass); bush: 1-2 sticks; stone: cobblestone by stone size (1-3); boulder: cobblestone 4-8, ore boulders add raw iron / raw copper / flint; walls, fences, doors: Minecraft blocks by material (brick wall → bricks, wood → planks, metal → iron bars or iron ingots, stone → cobblestone), and nothing for stone-like ones without a pickaxe (vanilla rule); windows: smashed on the first hit (PZ's smash, glass sound), the frame mined like a wall; furniture with `IsMoveAble`: PZ's own pick-up (the same `Moveable` item PZ gives, multi-tile pieces whole), which reaches Steve's inventory through the H4 mirror; a container's contents spill onto the floor first (as an explosion does); other furniture: material blocks. PZ's axes, pickaxes and sledgehammers count as Minecraft tools of their kind.
- **W3, placing furniture.** Right-click with a PZ furniture item on a PZ floor: PZ's own placement (`ISMoveableSpriteProps`, same validity rules), facing from Steve's yaw; the item leaves the inventory through the mirror. First pass: PZ's display name and a generic icon; then the sprite's own picture as the icon.
- **W4, seats and beds.** Right-click a seat (`SeatingManager` knows it): Steve rides an invisible seat entity at PZ's sit point, facing PZ's sit direction (couches: each tile a seat); Shift stands up (vanilla dismount). Right-click a PZ bed: vanilla's sleeping rules (night or thunder, no monsters near: PZ zombie proxies count), the night skips (PZ's clock follows, as with Minecraft beds today), and the respawn point is set beside the bed (forced, so it holds without a Minecraft bed block). Needs mixins on `checkBedExists` / the sleep position.
- **W5, cars.** Right-click a car door area: PZ seats the puppet (driver if free, else the best seat), Steve keeps owning survival (change `SteveControl`, which today ends Steve mode in a car), Steve rides an invisible seat entity that follows PZ's seat position every tick (PZ is authoritative for position while seated: `PuppetDriver` turns around), PZ drives with its own controls, physics, keys (the mirror gives PZ Steve's keys), fuel and engine; crash damage reaches Steve through `HealthLink`; Shift gets out (PZ exits and places the puppet, Steve is put there). Car windows are W0 targets: a punch runs PZ's `VehicleWindow.hit`/`damage` with PZ's sound until it breaks (the way into a locked car). Viewpoint's camera follows the PZ player, so the in-car view is Viewpoint's.

**Order (recommended):** W1 (small, independent) → W0 + W2 on grass, bushes and stones (proves the target and mining path on simple objects) → W2 walls, fences, doors, windows → W2 + W3 furniture → W4 → W5 (largest). Each step: built on the VPS, then the owner tests on maple; nothing counts as verified before that.

**Before code:** the uncommitted 2026-10-16 bug-fix batch (B2, B11-B17, resume bullet 11:40) should be committed first, or W work goes on its own branch, so the two are not mixed.

### Earlier open work to retain

- Native water transparency/refraction; PZ actor immersion, currents/buoyancy and durable passenger rebinding after save/reload.
- Boats rowing and minecart rail travel with native passengers; multi-seat/destruction/death/reconnect coverage; animal bounds and moving/rotating vehicle collision.
- Dead-save reload/manual MC Respawn case without competing user input. Existing features-reload-results.json is inconclusive due to manual play, not a persistence pass/failure.
- Crawler, multiple-zombie, fast-motion, wall and third-person combat checks; remote redstone/early-proxy edge coverage from older plans.
- Blocked/upper-floor beds, stairs and upper-storey movement; locked/barricaded doors/windows; partial fluid barriers, buckets and wall-light occlusion; unusual entities/renderers.
- TNT entity shielding, cars/fires, upper floors, jumbo trees, zombie pit behavior, long endurance and shader-pack compatibility.
- Optional performance work only when measurements justify it: HUD dirty rectangles/GPU sharing; streamer idle/read-ahead/main-thread budgets. Minimized Minecraft remains the default. The faint temporal trail is closed.

## 7. Architecture and source map

| Area | PZ / shared sources | Minecraft sources |
|---|---|---|
| Link/input | protocol InputReader, Session, Wire; PZ LinkService, InputForwarder, GuiInput | PzInput, InputBridge, FrameHook, PzCraftClient, GuiBridge |
| Terrain/collision | WorldExporter, BlockBridge, vehicle snapshots; protocol Coords/Collision | CollisionField, TerrainCoverage, TerrainNavigationMixin, TerrainWaypointMixin, terrain/path/support mixins |
| Camera/survival | SteveCamera, SteveEye, SteveControl, SteveVitals, PuppetDriver, HealthLink | camera/respawn mixins, ActorProxies, RespawnBridge |
| Rendering | NativeBlocks, NativeEntities, Overlay; protocol SceneLink/EntityLink | BlockSceneExporter, EntitySceneExporter, EntityCapture, FluidCapture, native/visibility/particle mixins |
| Time/objects/environment | ClockBridge, InteractionBridge, LightBridge, PassengerBridge, weather patches | TimeBridge, InteractionBridge, EnvironmentBridge, PassengerBridge, weather/clock/use mixins |
| Materials/trees/blasts/ground | PzMaterials, TreeBridge, ExplosionBridge, GroundBridge; protocol Materials, MaterialSection, PzExplosion | MaterialField, PzTreeBlock, PzBlocks, PzCraftMain, TreeBridge, ExplosionBridge, GroundBridge, ExplosionMixin |
| Items/containers/UI | ItemExporter, ContainerBridge, HudBridge; protocol ItemRules and Wire | PzItemCatalog, PzItemPack, PzPackSource, PzItems, ContainerBridge, PackRepositoryMixin |

Sources are under `pz-mod/src/main/java/pzcraft/pz`, `fabric-mod/src/main/java/pzcraft/mc`, its mixin subdirectory, and `protocol/src/main/java/pzcraft/protocol`. Confirm exact names before editing; some older plans use obsolete class names.

Key implementation facts:

- One tile = one MC block horizontally; one PZ level = approximately **2.449489742783178** MC blocks vertically. Use `Coords`, not a rounded three-block storey.
- Viewpoint's deferred path is world → g-buffer → light → composition. Native MC geometry is inserted before lighting and uses the current Viewpoint camera/depth. Scene x/z axes are negative while light-grid lookup coordinates remain positive.
- Direct input reads shared memory where MC consumes movement/actions/look. Polling threads still service events/heartbeat; ping RTT cannot measure visual/movement latency.
- SharedLink remains v3; EntityLink is v4 after the 2026-10-04 item-atlas capacity fix. Verify current constants before any protocol change. Body and final camera pose must be from the same EntityLink frame. Update both halves together.
- Message ids 59 materials, 60 tree fell, 61 explosion, 62 ground edits, **63 container open, 64 contents, 65 commit** are allocated. Terrain-column readiness uses id 13; GUI 46/47, lifecycle/clock/interact/light/passengers 48–56, weather 57/58.
- MC ground is virtual until approached/dug/blasted. Per-column depth is stored atomically with MC chunks; PZ stores removed-floor sprite/mod data. PZ tree proxy blocks use quiet placement/removal so bridge bookkeeping does not simulate player mining.
- `pzcraft:pz_item` represents many PZ types using per-stack custom data/components. State keys include `c` condition, `a` age, `k` cooked, `u` uses, `nm` custom name, `d` departure world day, `b` whole saved item bytes and `i` item identity. Keep grouping/matching rules consistent on both sides.
- Whole-item serialization uses PZ `saveWithSize` / `loadItem` with the current world version; byte payloads are opaque to Minecraft. Stateful blob stacks are non-stackable. Partial scalar reconstruction is not a substitute for bag/fluid/clothing state.
- PzItems food currently uses PZ hunger/thirst values to create vanilla food/consumable components, stale/rotten adjustments, hunger/nausea effects and use remainders. Clothing maps PZ body locations to MC slots and protection to armor; it currently disables damage-on-hurt for those equippables. Full PZ item behavior is not finished.
- Invisible, silent, AI-less MC proxies represent native actors. Runtime ids are not durable saved identities. Native PZ player rendering is suppressed while Steve drives.

### Prior failures worth remembering

- Vanilla air-column navigation in the void world selected the build ceiling; keep PZ surface/waypoint navigation overrides.
- Mod registration must happen in the main entrypoint, before registries freeze; the client entrypoint was too late for custom blocks/items.
- PZ `IsoObject` two-argument fixtures can create texture-only objects; use the proper constructor/native properties for collision tests.
- Vanilla single-player did not originally expose save-all. PzCraft registers the integrated-server save command; confirm `Minecraft world saved` after `mc save-all flush`.
- Fixed-function texture pollution could blacken the overlay; its own shader/state restoration is required. Native particle transparency uses dithered coverage resolved by TAA.
- MC 26.3 undead sunlight burning occurs through `Mob.isSunBurnTick`; cached vanilla eye height and stale proxy removal caused earlier fluid/entity problems.
- A much older staged ActorProxies compile error was later superseded by successful builds. Do not resurrect that historical blocker as the current status.
- Read logs from the current run/offset. Old exceptions remain in append-only logs and cannot diagnose the live build by themselves.

## 8. Commands and safe install/test procedure

Run from the workspace in PowerShell. Prefer the wrappers and pinned local JDK; do not install another loader/runtime to solve a stale-state problem.

```powershell
$env:JAVA_HOME = 'C:/Program Files/Microsoft/jdk-25.0.4.101-hotspot'
$env:PATH = "$env:JAVA_HOME/bin;$env:PATH"
$pzPython = 'C:/Users/Maple/AppData/Local/Python/pythoncore-3.14-64/python.exe'
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/pzctl.py procs
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/pzctl.py state
```

When code changes require a rebuild/install:

1. Check current manual-play intent. Announce any launch/restart or focus-taking step.
2. Save PZ (`saveGame()`) and MC (`mc save-all flush`), temporarily unpausing if necessary and restoring pause-on-focus-loss settings. Require confirmation for both before stopping. `pzcore.save_worlds()` confirms MC's log; inspect failures rather than killing anyway.
3. Stop exact game PIDs and confirm they are gone. **Never replace a running PZ jar or rewrite the direct-launch classpath while MC is using it.** Back up both worlds, installed mod/config and any source requiring rollback.
4. Execute these separately and stop on the first nonzero exit code:

```powershell
& ./protocol/gradlew.bat -p protocol test --offline --console=plain
& ./shim/gradlew.bat -p shim build --offline --console=plain
& ./fabric-mod/gradlew.bat -p fabric-mod build prepareDirectLaunch --offline --no-configuration-cache --console=plain
& ./pz-mod/gradlew.bat -p pz-mod build installMod --offline --console=plain
```

5. Compare built/installed PZ jar and mod resources by hash. Do not rely on `scripts/install.bat`'s final `done` alone: it does not provide a dependable failure gate or stopped-process check.
6. Normal player launch is `scripts/start-all.bat` / `scripts/run-pz.bat`, with Steam running. Since 2026-10-04 the launcher uses the player's ZombieBuddy (2.3.4+, which fixes 42.21 itself) and **no shim** unless `PZ_USE_SHIM=1`; the launcher copies the latest successful PZ jar only while PZ is stopped. Normal MC launch is direct Java with a 2 GB heap cap; run-minecraft-dev.bat is the separate Gradle development route. `pzctl launch` unpauses the world at its end: pause it again. `pzctl kill` saves both worlds (unpausing PZ for the save) and refuses to stop anything if a save is not confirmed (`--force` overrides).
7. Use the normal ZombieBuddy approval policy. A changed jar may need the owner's approval. The existing private `~/.pzcraft/zb-dev` profile has saved approvals and can be reused with `PZ_ZB_ARGS=config_dir=...`; avoid blanket allow-all solely for convenience. User's normal `~/.zombie_buddy/mod_approvals.json` is separate.
8. Load the named development save only for tests. Verify linked/ready state and rendering failure counters; run the smallest relevant check. Save/cleanup, record evidence here, and hand manual control back.

The long-lived `pz` MCP server can expose stale Python process-detection code. The CLI imports current pzcore.py and correctly recognizes direct launches. Re-test the actual API/CLI before diagnosing missing integration.

**Do not run scripts/test-walk.bat or another fake-PZ harness while real PZ is running**: they use the same shared-memory slots. Prefer API input over desktop automation. `pzctl respawn` creates a new PZ character; it is not the MC Respawn button and should not be used for MC death-screen validation.

### Relevant repeatable checks (only during authorized development)

```powershell
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/check-containers.py --kind object --state --blobs
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/check-containers.py --kind object --min-containers 2
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/check-containers.py --kind corpse
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/check-containers.py --kind floor
& $pzPython tools/pz-mcp/check-items.py
```

Older regressions: check-camera.py, check-moodles.py, check-render-weather.py (eye/moving/boat/particles/weather), check-trees.py, check-tnt.py, check-ground.py dig/crater, check-speed.py elytra --seconds 30, check-native-012.py loading/effects, check-headless.py minimized --seconds 30. Read each script's prerequisites/cleanup before running. The inventory scripts borrow slots, seed items and may replace armor; revise/isolate unsafe fixtures before applying them to an owner's used character.

Feature-stage runner: `check-features.py` with lifecycle, distant, upper, doors, environment, fluids, redstone, entities, transport or animals; check-reach.py covers reach/pillar. Native controls checks may take desktop focus. Generated screenshots are local verification evidence, not an excuse to report uninspected visuals as passes.

## 9. Evidence and log index

| Location | Use |
|---|---|
| `E:/Zomboid/console.txt` | Current PZ console; [PzCraft] link/pose/input diagnostics, Java/Lua errors and native renderer counters |
| `pz-launch.log` | Append-only PZ launcher, shim/ZombieBuddy mod loading/approval output; find the current launch marker |
| `fabric-run-live.log` | Direct MC startup, frame/bridge logs, catalogue/load/save confirmation and exceptions |
| `tools/pz-mcp/*-results.json` | Test assertions/outcome/cleanup; inspect passed or stage-specific pass/error fields, not filename alone |
| `tools/pz-mcp/*-samples.json` | Measurement samples; needed to assess performance distribution rather than one screenshot |
| `tools/pz-mcp/*.png` | Screenshots/visual test evidence; keep exact associated test/build/time |
| `protocol/build/test-results/test/TEST-*.xml` | Existing unit-test totals, failures and timestamps |
| `fabric-run.log`, fabric-run2/3/4.log, fabric-native-test*.log | Earlier startup/harness runs; historical unless tied to the current investigation |
| `walk-run*.log`, gensources.log | Original physics harness/source-generation history; avoid confusing them with current live runs |
| Documentation ZIP in section 4 | Complete pre-consolidation plans, validation narratives and MODLOG for deeper historical retrieval |

Do not concatenate large runtime logs or binary/base64 item data into this file. Add short dated observations and exact evidence paths. Preserve raw logs and results; no runtime logs were moved, truncated or deleted during consolidation.

### Performance observations with their limits

- 0.12 idle measured median HUD overlay age ~29.3 → **17.5 ms**, estimated read-back ~565 → **289 MiB/s**, one HUD layer and no video entities. This is an idle measurement, not movement latency.
- Minimized versus permanently hidden MC: essentially the same latency; hidden CPU ~58% versus minimized ~39% of one core. Keep minimized as default. Hidden creation still avoids the startup flash.
- 0.12 flight samples observed PZ ~33–39 FPS as the mesh budget rose; 0.13 final elytra run reported median ~58.8 FPS. Different runs/scenes are not a controlled direct comparison or a universal frame-rate claim.
- Removing runtime Gradle reduced MC working memory in the earlier D1 idle measurement; later renderer/read-back changes did not demonstrate a memory improvement. Preserve the distinction.

Diagnosis (evidence = `containers-object-results-20261002-225537-FAILED-contaminated-fixture.json`, a byte copy of the 22:55 record): the put-back phase of `take_and_return` shift-clicked **every** inventory slot matching `Hammer|Apple|Lighter`, not just the stacks it had taken. `stateCarried` lists 8 stacks for 3 seeded items; the other 5 (a fresh Apple, a fresh Hammer, `My Hammer (Worn)`, a second Apple a=1.50, a second Lighter u=0.406) were already in Steve's pockets, evidently leftovers of the earlier aborted 22:37/22:40 runs. The same flaw existed in the first phase (put back every stack of the taken types) and in the cleanup (`mc clear @s minecraft:cobblestone` removed *all* of Steve's cobblestone). Fix: items are now moved back only from the slots that filled during the take (`pz_slots`/`arrived`); a stack that merged into one Steve already carries stops the test instead of moving the owner's items; phase 1 only takes kinds Steve does not already carry; the cobblestone check uses before/after counts and cleanup clears exactly 3; the initial `CLEAN` of the fixture container is now a guard that refuses to delete pre-existing fixture-type items; `check_state`/`check_blobs` count per type (a duplicate no longer hides in a dict); the state round trip asserts the container held exactly 3 fixture items; new result keys `*AlreadyCarried`, `*InventoryRestored`, `stateCleaned`, `tookSlots`; every run also writes `containers-<kind>-results-<timestamp>.json`.
Validation: `py_compile` OK. Offline self-test on the exact contaminated data from the failed record: only slots 5–7 selected, a merge raises, the failed `stateAfter` is now rejected and a clean round trip passes. **This validates the test logic only; no gameplay rerun yet.**

Leftovers: Steve's saved inventory probably still holds the stray Apples/Hammers/Lighter from earlier aborted runs (not deleted; they could be test leftovers or the owner's items).

Relaunched 09:38 with `PZ_ZB_ARGS=config_dir=~/.pzcraft/zb-dev` (saved approvals, no allow-all).
Evidence (**verified** for this launch, not a long soak): pz-launch.log `[ZB] activating ZombieBuddy v2.3.4`, no `PzCraftShim` line; zbNative swapped the game-folder jar itself (`ZombieBuddy.jar.new` consumed; jar now 13,254,999 bytes = workshop v2.3.4) — this is the normal player upgrade path; `[ZB] Found patch class … Patch_ZomboidFileSystem$Patch_loadMods2` (the upstream 42.21 fix); one `[PzCraft] PzCraft 0.13.0 loading` line (no double load), Viewpoint 3D on, link ready, HUD hiding 0 failures; the save loaded without the missing-mod prompt because PZ rewrote `mods.txt` at the 09:23 load (no TemporaryFix entry any more); Steve's inventory identical (28 occupied slots) after the stop/start. **My mistake and the fix:** at 09:36:46 `pzctl kill` reported "Minecraft save not confirmed" and killed both games anyway. Cause: a paused PZ pauses Minecraft's integrated server (log: "Saving and pausing game…" at 09:28:38/09:30:18), and a paused server never runs `save-all`, so the command sat queued. The last real Minecraft save was the 09:30:18 pause-save, after the final test, so no test state was lost, and world files and Steve's inventory loaded cleanly afterwards; but the 09:36:59 force-kill could in principle have cut a write. HANDOFF §8 already said "require confirmation… temporarily unpausing if necessary"; the tool did not. Fixed in `tools/pz-mcp/pzcore.py`/`pzctl.py`: `save_worlds` now unpauses PZ for the save (and re-pauses), `kill` refuses (returns `refused`) unless every running half confirmed its save, `--force` overrides, and `launch(restart=True)` stops instead of starting a second PZ when refused. Compiled; not yet exercised with a real refusal.
Game/save now: PZ (23456) + MC (24048), **running, paused, linked**, Steve (2157.43, 6110.95). `pzctl launch` ends by unpausing the world: pause it again after launching.

### 2026-10-04 10:00–10:25 CST — Claude — H4 mirror: implemented and compile-checked (NOT installed, NOT tested in game yet)

Status of everything below: **implemented**, **compile-checked** (isolated `build-check` outputs, `--init-script scripts/check-build.gradle`, the running games' `build/` outputs untouched); protocol unit tests **passed** (8 new `MirrorTest` + the existing suite; `gradlew -p protocol test` run 10:02). Not built into the installed jars, not exercised in game. The design is in §6 "H4 design note".
New/changed files:
- `protocol/.../Mirror.java` (key function, count delta/apply/target) + `MirrorTest.java`; `Wire.java`: `MSG_MIRROR_STATE=66`, `MSG_MIRROR_TX=67`, `MSG_MIRROR_HELLO=68`.
- PZ: `MirrorBridge.java` (ledger saved in player mod data `PzCraftMirror`, scan every 0.5 s while Steve drives, one acknowledged transaction in flight, in-place replace via structural signature, stand-ins `PzCraft.MCItem`, `localOnly` kinds Minecraft rejects, stats `pzMirror`), wired in `LinkService`, `FrameTick`, `Control`; `ContainerBridge.java`: item serialization helpers widened to package visibility and item creation extracted into `create(entry, now)` (behaviour intended unchanged; **re-run the container checks after install as a regression**); `pz-mod/mod/42/media/scripts/pzcraft_items.txt` (the `PzCraft.MCItem` item, `hidden = true`).
- Minecraft: `MirrorBridge.java` (server-tick snapshot every 4 ticks, state messages, transaction apply with a persisted ring of applied ids as a Fabric attachment `pzcraft:mirror_applied`, overflow drops at Steve's feet, stats under `minecraftGui.mirror`), wired in `PzCraftMain`, `PzCraftClient`, `GuiBridge`.
- Test: `tools/pz-mcp/check-mirror.py` (phases A–E, see its docstring) + baselines `mirror-baseline-mc-inventory-20261004.txt` and `mirror-baseline-pz-pockets-20261004.txt` (before-mirror: PZ pockets = `Base.IDcard`, `Base.KeyRing` containing `Base.Key1`; Steve = 28 occupied slots).
Bugs found by reading my own draft before compiling (fixed): (1) an item PZ could not create looked like a PZ-side removal and would have been deleted from Minecraft — the ledger now holds only what the pockets actually hold from Minecraft's state; (2) item age had been stripped from outgoing entries (stale food would turn fresh) — age travels but is not part of the key; (3) a Minecraft-rejected PZ item would later have been deleted from PZ — such kinds become `localOnly`; (4) the rejected-key scan ran before the ack was processed — reordered; (5) float vs double rounding of uses in the key — made identical to `ContainerBridge`.
Known limitations (by design for this stage): worn/equipped state not mirrored; ageing differences inside a stack of equal key are not tracked; a food item partly eaten inside a bag is not detected as a change (the structural signature ignores hunger/age); a Minecraft stack that cannot be written as text or exceeds the 150 KB message budget stays Minecraft-only (counted in `skipped`/`unencodable`); a paused PZ pauses Minecraft's server so nothing syncs while paused.
Next: stop the games safely (new `pzctl kill`), back up both worlds, build + install (§8), relaunch (ZombieBuddy will ask to approve the changed `PzCraft.jar`: the owner clicks Yes), run `check-mirror.py` and the container/items regression checks, record results here.

### 2026-10-04 10:00–10:45 CST — Claude — mirror installed; first results; **two real losses found and explained**; fixes in progress

Installed (09:58–10:00): protocol tests pass; PZ jar sha256 `4c1bae937aefb335…` (305,223 bytes) installed = built, `mod.info` and `media/scripts/pzcraft_items.txt` match, Fabric 0.13.0 jar rebuilt with `MirrorBridge`; backup `.pzcraft-backups/before-mirror-install-20261004-0958/` (both worlds, installed mod, old jars). ZombieBuddy approval for the new jar was given by the owner (private dev profile, approvals 53→54).
First sync (verified in game): union as designed — Steve received `IDcard` and `KeyRing`(+`Key1`), PZ received 30 items (12 PZ items, 18 stand-ins). `check-mirror.py` phases A (projection), B (quiet), C (Minecraft→PZ), D (PZ→Minecraft incl. in-place key-ring change in its own slot), E passed on the third run (`mirror-results-20261004-100254.json`); the two earlier failed runs were test-script bugs (Lua prints big ints in exponent form; regex missed ` whole`), their records are kept as `…FAILED-*.json`. Container regression after the `ContainerBridge` refactor: object state/blobs, fridge, corpse passed; floor failed once at the first open ("timed out waiting for the container screen", nothing taken) and later passed 7 more times.
**Losses (owner's dev character, found at ~10:07):** (1) the **repeater** from hotbar slot 8, (2) Steve's **worn Police Jacket** (`Base.Jacket_Police`, a stateful whole-item stack), and (3) hotbar stacks (elytra, flint-and-steel, TNT, raw copper) were moved from slots 3–6 into slots 27–32. Evidence of state before: `mirror-baseline-*.txt`, `mirror-state-before-clear-20261004.json`; the 09:58 backup holds the originals.
Causes found:
- *The jacket* (**reproduced under the watcher** with a seeded jacket): `check-items.py` parks the worn chest piece by copying it into a spare slot, then clearing the chest. For about half a second Minecraft held **two identical jackets (same PZ item id)**. The mirror saw count 2 and tried to create a second PZ item; **PZ cannot hold two items with one id, `AddItem` silently did not add it, but `conform` counted it as created**. The next scan found one jacket fewer than the ledger and sent a *removal* transaction; Minecraft removed the original. Both gone (watcher: removal tx at 4.14 s, slot 27 emptied 4.30 s). A real mirror defect, not only a test artefact.
- *The repeater* (strong evidence, not reproduced as a loss): the selected hotbar slot was **8**, holding the repeater, during the container/floor/corpse runs. Those tests press the **use key while looking at the ground**; with a placeable item in hand Minecraft uses *that item* instead of opening the PZ container (the floor check timing out at the first open fits this, and it failed twice more on the fixture with the repeater selected again). A test-safety bug of mine: the tests never chose a harmless hand.
- *Moved hotbar stacks:* not conclusively explained; the likely path is remove-then-add churn (below) around the failed `check-items` run, whose parked-jacket restore crashed (`unpark_chest` timeout) and left the chest wrong (the elytra ended in the chest slot). Treat as unexplained.
**Owner instruction received during this work (chat): "clear the item first so you can test fully."** I read this as: empty Steve's inventory so the tests are not constrained by leftover/owner items. Done at 10:12: `mc clear @s` with the world running; the mirror cleared the PZ pockets with it (**including the character's `IDcard` and `KeyRing`+`Key1`**, which are recoverable from the 09:58 backup and the baseline files). The fixture is now rebuilt by `tools/pz-mcp/seed-mirror-fixture.py` (Minecraft-only hotbar items, `/pzgive`n PZ items, PZ-side ID card + key ring). If "the item" meant something narrower, say so; nothing else was deleted.
Fixes written (**implemented and compile-checked, not yet installed**):
- PZ `MirrorBridge`: after `AddItem` it verifies PZ really holds the item, otherwise marks the kind `refused` (extra copy stays Minecraft-only, logged); the ledger is taken from a **fresh scan of the pockets**, never from what `conform` meant to do; a key change of the same stateful item (condition, name, plain `/pzgive` stack that gains its PZ id) is sent as an **in-place replace** instead of remove+add, so slots no longer shuffle; every transaction is logged (PZ console and Minecraft log).
- Tests: `pzcore.select_inert_hand()`/`restore_hand()` (container tests now pick an empty hotbar slot or a plain tool before pressing use; `selectedSlot` is now in the GUI state); `check-items.py` cleanup is per-step with errors recorded and marks the run failed if the chest piece is not back; `check-mirror.py` gained **phase F** (the duplicate-window scenario above, must end with exactly one jacket on both sides); new diagnostics `watch-mirror.py` and `seed-mirror-fixture.py`; `check-mirror-restart.py` (record / change / compare) for the persistence and crash cases.
Second install (10:23, jar `5e05cc79…`, approved) results: clean-stop **persistence verified once** — after save/kill/relaunch Steve's 30 slots were identical and PZ loaded its ledger (29 kinds) and needed to create/remove nothing; the in-place swap worked (plain `IDcard`/`Apple` became whole items as `0 added, 0 removed, 2 changed` without leaving their slots). `check-mirror.py` A–E passed again but **phase F failed and exposed a flaw in my own fix**: building the ledger from a fresh scan lost the link between Minecraft's key for a plain `/pzgive` stack and the key of the whole item PZ makes from it, so PZ **created and dropped a jacket every ~0.5 s** (watcher: created 103→116, removed 101→114 in 6 s, each with a new item id; no transactions were sent, nothing was lost, test jacket removed afterwards). Fix (third build, jar `9f8efcdc…7ebc`, installed 10:31, **launched; waiting for the owner's ZombieBuddy approval at the time of writing**): `conform` returns (Minecraft key, own key) for every item it made and the ledger keeps Minecraft's key for it, so the next round swaps in place; plus a **circuit breaker** (more than 300 items made or dropped within 10 s stops the mirror, status `STOPPED…`, counted in `failures`, logged `ERROR inventory mirror stopped itself`).
Still open for H4: reinstall with the fixes and re-run A–F + container/items regression on the fixture; the restart cases (clean stop, PZ crash, Minecraft crash, PZ-only play merge); a second long-term question — Minecraft can create PZ-typed stacks that PZ cannot mirror one-to-one (duplicates), see `refused`.

### 2026-10-04 10:25–11:00 CST — Claude — H4 mirror: fixes installed, restart/rollback cases verified (summary; details in the entries above)

Third build installed 10:31 (PZ jar `9f8efcdc…`, approved by the owner at the ZombieBuddy dialog; the launcher had timed out waiting for that click, a second `pzctl launch` finished loading). Fixes since the second build: (1) after `AddItem` PZ verifies it holds the item (PZ cannot hold two items with one id → `refused`, logged, extra copy stays Minecraft-only); (2) the ledger comes from a fresh scan **but keeps Minecraft's key for items PZ made for it** (`made` aliases) — without that the plain-`/pzgive` case made PZ create and drop a jacket every 0.5 s (found by phase F, no loss); (3) plain→whole key changes and condition/name changes go as **in-place replace** (no slot moves); (4) **circuit breaker**: more than 300 items made or dropped in 10 s stops the mirror (`STOPPED…`, counted as a failure); (5) every transaction is logged on both sides; (6) tests: inert hand before the use key (verified selection via `pzcore._select_slot`, `selectedSlot` in the GUI state), `give()` takes its item back out if it fails after the item landed, `check-items.py` cleanup is per-step and fails the run if the chest piece is not back.

In particular S2b/S3 show the intended crash behaviour: **Minecraft is canonical, PZ follows**; a PZ-side rollback re-creates what Minecraft has (no duplicate, no transaction), a Minecraft rollback makes PZ drop what Minecraft no longer has (as a Minecraft crash would), and PZ-only changes merge exactly once on reconnect.

Category counts: blunt 58, smallblade 56, improvised+smallblunt 52, smallblunt 46, axe 41, blunt+improvised 38, improvised+spear 20, longblade 14, spear 9, unarmed 1; maxHitCount is 1–3 for melee and 0 for thrown.

PZ rule reproduced: one swing hits at most `maxHitCount` targets and the n-th takes `damage / n` of the first target's damage. ### 2026-10-04 16:20–16:32 CST — Claude — restart ammo loss: root cause found, fixed, verified

**Root cause:** `InventoryItem.load` calls `setCustomName(bits.hasFlags(64))`, and `setCustomName` always writes `modData.customName` (even for false). So after any PZ save/load every item has non-empty mod data, and `ContainerBridge.needsBlob` (`hasModData() && !isEmpty()`) made every item travel as a whole single item. Plain stacks of identical items (the owner's 64 rounds, TinOpener, CannedCornedBeef) were therefore split into one-item whole stacks on the first reload, which cannot fit into Minecraft's 36 slots; the overflow was lost (first restart at 16:03: `rem Bullets9mm n=64` with the whole replacements refused; second restart 16:23: 64 whole one-round stacks, only 23 of them kept in PZ's pockets, 41 destroyed, none on the floor). This is older than the magazine work (any restart since the mirror existed would have done it to a stack once it had been through a save).
**Fix (installed):** `ContainerBridge.hasRealModData` ignores the key `customName`; PZ `PzCraft.jar` sha256 `c9ad6e73f5eafc7de4e78f018062a2bae99b6cc732758050ae19186b90f4f5c1` (335292 B, built = installed, approved with the helper, backup `.pzcraft-backups/pzcraft-approvals-*-c9ad6e73.json`). Fabric unchanged (`afb19a04…`).
**Verified:** (1) the first restart with the fix consolidated the 23 stray whole rounds (and the plain TinOpener and CannedCornedBeef) back into plain stacks by itself (PZ console: `loaded ledger of 36 kinds`, one transaction removing the whole keys and adding the plain ones; mirror in step, no failures; evidence `tools/pz-mcp/ammo-persistence-after-fix-20261004.json`); (2) I gave back the 41 lost rounds and moved the stacks back to the owner's slots; (3) a clean full restart from that state (`persistence2-before-20261004.json` vs `persistence2-after-20261004.json`, backup `.pzcraft-backups/before-persistence2-20261004-162800/`): Minecraft inventory identical, PZ counts identical (64 rounds), same PZ item ids, no mirror transaction. New helper `tools/pz-mcp/snapshot-inventory.py FILE`.
**Owner inventory now (16:32), as at the 15:59 baseline:** slots 0–5 vanilla starter stacks (selected slot 6 is empty), slot 10 Sledgehammer, 11 Katana, 12 TinOpener, 13 CannedCornedBeef, 14 **64 Bullets9mm**, 15 empty 9mmClip, 16 Pistol (unloaded), 33 Bag_AmmoBox_9mm. ### 2026-10-04 17:45–18:00 CST — Claude — W2 done: PZ's own weapon meshes in the hands (installed; seen in first and third person)

**Owner's complaint:** held PZ weapons were flat extruded icons ("katana is like an ordinary item visually"). **No Blender/Minebench/downloads were needed:** PZ ships every weapon as a small text DirectX `.x` mesh with a PNG texture, read read-only from the game install; the converted data lives only in `~/.pzcraft/items/weapon-models/` (private, never in the project or a jar).
**Pipeline:** `tools/pz-mcp/wm_parse.py` (item scripts `media/scripts/generated/items/weapon.txt` -> `WeaponSprite`/`WeaponSpritesByIndex`, model scripts `models_*.txt` -> mesh/texture/scale/attachments) + `tools/pz-mcp/build-weapon-models.py` (uses `xmesh.py`; writes `index.json` {items fullType -> model key, models}, `models/<key>.json` (triangles, uv, smooth normals, raw .x coordinates), `textures/<key>.png`, a `display.json` written once). Result: **371 weapons -> 364 models**; skipped 37 (32 whose mesh is not a text .x: bombs/traps `WorldItems/*`, `Base.JS3T_Shotgun` mesh has no geometry, `Base.BareHands`, the two cap guns have no model script); `Base.Pistol` is excluded (the M9 keeps its GeckoLib model). Fabric: `PzWeaponModels.java` (registers the special item model type `pzcraft:pzmesh` by putting its codec into the widened `SpecialModelRenderers.ID_MAPPER` at client init, no mixin; loads the meshes; `writePack` adds textures `item/pzmodel/<key>.png`, `models/item/pzmodel_base.json` and one `items/pzw/<itemtype>.json` per weapon = `minecraft:select` on `display_context`: gui/ground/fixed/on_shelf keep the PZ sprite, everything else (hands, frames of Steve) = the mesh; the renderer submits triangles as quads through `submitCustomGeometry` with `RenderTypes.entityCutout`); `PzItemPack` stamp includes `PzWeaponModels.stamp()` (index + display.json mtime/size) so a re-run of the converter rebuilds the pack; `PzItems.stack` sets `ITEM_MODEL = pzcraft:pzw/<type>`; an every-2-seconds server tick `PzWeaponModels.retarget` repoints stacks made before (the owner's katana/sledgehammer/shotgun) without touching their data (inventory strings and the mirror unchanged, mirror "in step").
**Placement (found by looking, live-tunable in `~/.pzcraft/tuning.json`; file is `{}` = shipped defaults):** `pzmesh.*` (first person) and `pzmesh3.*` (third person, selected by `options.getCameraType().isFirstPerson()` because the special renderer is not told the display context): `scale` 3.0 (the held pose shrinks everything ~3x relative to block units), `x/y/z`, `ry` roll about the blade axis, `rx` tip forward, `rz` lean (vertex order: roll, tip, lean), `flipX 1 / flipZ -1` (.x is left-handed; winding reversed to match). Defaults: first person rx -30, ry 90, rz 35, x .35, y .05, z -.3; third person rx 30, ry 0, rz 90, y -.25 (the third-person hand frame has the blade at identity pointing sideways from the hand: rz 90 turns it forward; the arm hangs). PZ's `Bip01_Prop2` hand attachments are NOT applied (one transform for all weapons; looks right on all checked ones). Helper `tools/pz-mcp/tune_shot.py NAME key=val ...` (bare keys get the `pzmesh.` prefix; `reset=1`).
**Seen (screenshots in `m9-views/`):** `sheet-mesh-swing.png` (Katana real Better Combat swing, curved blade, tsuba and wrapped handle; the handle looks big when the slash passes close to the camera), `sheet-weapons1.png` (first person: Sledgehammer, BaseballBat, Axe (red fire axe), Machete, Shotgun (long barrel, edge-on), Hammer, Crowbar (blue), KitchenKnife: all PZ models with their PZ textures), third person `sheet-tp10.png` (katana pointing forward from the hand, front view; the back view hides it behind the owner's note-block structure), hotbar/inventory still show the PZ sprites. **Not verified:** third person from a clean open scene (both Viewpoint cameras are partly blocked by the owner's note blocks next to Steve; tried turning Steve, the camera did not follow); left-handed use; items with `WeaponSpritesByIndex` variants (first sprite only); weapon parts (scopes/bayonets, `ModelWeaponPart`); firearms other than the M9 are only models (no gun mechanics: they are held like melee weapons); ground drops and item frames show the sprite by design.

Owner (via the parent session): "still having 20% issues, mostly bugs": priority other mods (Viltrumite), block-breaking animation looks bad, cannot use GUI shortcuts (shift+click), much 26.3 code not converted, M16 and most guns' model/animation broken. **Causes and changes** (version-local unless noted):
- Other mods' keys: in world only movement/attack/use/drop/hotbar and ~8 hard-coded keys reached Minecraft. `ModKeys` (1.20.1 override; shared stub in `src` for 26.x) publishes `modKeys` (GLFW keys of every non-vanilla KeyMapping, minus keys PZ drives Steve with) in the GUI state; `pz-mod GuiInput` forwards press/repeat/release of those keys while Steve drives (after PZ's own gun/G/E/T keys); `GuiBridge` (shared) hands them to the bindings (setDown + click). Vanilla bindings are found by identity (Options fields): field names are obfuscated in production, the first name-based attempt published nothing. `PzInput` (1.20.1) mirrors up/down/left/right/jump/sneak into the vanilla KeyMappings (Viltrumite polls keyJump/keyShift).
- Viltrumite flight black screen: its `GameRendererMixin` loads `viltrumiteflight:shaders/post/sonic_boom.json` during flight; any GameRenderer post chain writes opaque alpha, hiding PZ. `PostEffect120Mixin` (MixinExtras @WrapWithCondition on GameRenderer.render → PostChain.process) skips it while PZ is connected and logs the name once.
- Shift-click: 1.20.1 `Screen.hasShiftDown()` = `InputConstants.isKeyDown(Minecraft's window, 340/344)`; keys come from PZ's window. `KeyDown120Mixin` (InputConstants.isKeyDown HEAD) answers from `ModKeys.held` (keys pressed in screens + modifier bits of the latest event; cleared when the screen closes).
- Cracks: chunk layers are cancelled for native blocks, but vanilla still drew crumbling into the empty transparent video layer. `Crumbling120Mixin` cancels `BlockRenderDispatcher.renderBreakingTexture` while native entities are exported; `BlockCracks120` exports each `LevelRenderer.destructionProgress` block's quads as FLAG_DECAL with a 10-stage strip texture (one of the 32 entity texture slots). AW: `KeyMapping.clickCount`, `LevelRenderer.destructionProgress`.
- Guns: `GunModels` loaded only m16a1; now all 11 manifest kinds. `pz_m9`'s builtin/entity model was written as `pz_gun_m9.json` (sprite kept). `GunRenderer120` placement per kind: mode 1 (camera-space, default) or 0 (authored display transforms; tested — rifles huge, pistols upright); pistols default x .30 y -.62 z -.85 scale .30 (shows the hand); rifles keep Codex's .40/-.85/-.85/.30. `/pzgunanim` registered once.

**Builds:** `gradlew build -Pmc1201 :mc1.20.1:build` (26.3, 26.1.2, 1.20.1) and `pz-mod build`, `protocol test` all BUILD SUCCESSFUL.

### 2026-10-18 12:00 CST — Claude side session: 1.20.1 PZ-mesh weapon path

Task (from the parent session): port 26.3's "PZ's own converted mesh + generic motion" weapon path to 1.20.1, so PZ weapons without a bundled own Geo model stop showing as flat sprites.

**Changes:**
- Shared `GunMeshAnim` and `PzWeaponMotion` now compile for 1.20.1 and are taken out of `versions/1.20.1/shared-excludes.txt`. `PzWeaponMotion` has one `//#if MC_1_20` line, because Java 17 has no `Math.clamp`.
- `versions/1.20.1/.../PzWeaponModels.java` used to be a stub and is now a real override. It reads the same `index.json`/`display.json`/`models`/`textures` and registers each mesh texture as a `DynamicTexture` straight from the private data (the 1.20.1 item pack is unchanged). It applies the display transform and the -0.5 centring, then uses the shared renderer's placement, Tuning keys (`pzgun.*`, `pzmesh.*`), melee motion, `GunMeshAnim` pose and ADS sight shift. It draws through `RenderType.entityCutout`.
- New `mixin/PzWeaponMesh120Mixin` (in `pzcraft.mixins.json` client): `ItemRenderer.render` HEAD hands PZ weapon stacks to `PzWeaponModels.render` and cancels the sprite. 1.20.1 stand-in for `pzcraft:pzmesh` and `WeaponModelContextMixin`.
- New `versions/1.20.1/.../PzWeaponArms.java`: same arm IK as the shared file. It draws the player model's arm and sleeve directly in rest pose plus the ±0.1 roll, after the mesh (shared immediate buffer). `renderLeftHand/RightHand` kept the gun's CROSSBOW_HOLD yaw from setupAnim.
